---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n366\t# Internal state + classification helpers\n367\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n368\t\n369\t\n370\t@dataclass\n371\tclass _LifecycleState:\n372\t    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n373\t\n374\t    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n375\t    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n376\t    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n377\t    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n378\t    \"\"\"\n379\t\n380\t    spec: EvalSpec\n381\t    observed: ExpectResult) -> None:\n390\t        self.expects.append(result)\n391\t\n392\t    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n393\t        self.harness_failure_step = step\n394\t        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n395\t\n396\t\n397\tdef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n398\t    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n399\t\n400\t    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n401\t\n402\t    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n403\t      fully-qualified classname.\n404\t    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n405\t    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n406\t\n407\t    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n408\t    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n409\t    \"\"\"\n410\t\n411\t    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n412\t        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n413\t        cls = type(exc)\n414\t        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n415\t\n416\t    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n417\t        return \"PASS\", None\n418\t\n419\t    return \"FAIL\", None\n420\t\n421\t\n422\tdef _finalize(\n423\t    *,\n424\t    state: _LifecycleState,\n425\t    home: HomeIsolation,\n426\t    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n427\t    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n428\t) -> EvalOutcome:\n429\t    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n430\t\n431\t    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n432\t    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n433\t Teardown failures are routed through\n435\t    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n436\t    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n437\t    \"\"\"\n438\t\n439\t    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n440\t\n441\t    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n442\t    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n443\t\n444\t    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n445\t    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n446\t    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n447\t    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n448\t    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n449\t        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n450\t        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n451\t        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n452\t        duration_sec = 0.0\n453\t\n454\t    return EvalOutcome(\n455\t        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n456\t        title=state.spec.title,\n457\t        status=status,\n458\t        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n459\t        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n460\t        skip_reason=None,\n461\t        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n462\t        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n463\t        error_class=error_class,\n464\t    )\n465\t\n466\t\n467\tdef _safe_teardown(\n468\t    home: HomeIsolation,\n469\t    *,\n470\t    keep: bool,\n471\t    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n472\t) -> None:\n473\t    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n474\t\n475\t    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n476\t    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n477\t    docstring). The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n812\t        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n813\t        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n814\t        branch this class adds.\n815\t\n816\t        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n817\t        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n818\t        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n819\t        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n820\t        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n821\t        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n822\t        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n823\t        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n824\t        \"\"\"\n825\t\n826\t        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n827\t        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n828\t\n829\t        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. makes a hung worker rare in production.\n892\t        thread = threading.Thread(\n893\t            target=worker,\n894\t            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n895\t            daemon=True,\n896\t        )\n897\t        thread.start()\n898\t        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n899\t\n900\t        if thread.is_alive():\n901\t            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n902\t\n903\t        # Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n904\t        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n905\t        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. at the suite layer.\n        _safe_teardown(home, keep=True, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n        raise\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal state + classification helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LifecycleState:\n    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n\n    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _NO_OBSERVED_RUN\n    expects: list[ExpectResult] = field(default_factory=list)\n    harness_failure_step: Optional[str] = None\n    harness_failure_exc: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,\n    keep: bool,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n\n    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n    docstring). The optional callback lets COMP-004 (T03.05) record\n    teardown failures into its per-eval JSONL log without re-raising\n    from the lifecycle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        home.teardown(keep=keep)\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        raise\n    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — swallowed per spec\n        if on_teardown_error is not None:\n            try:\n                on_teardown_error(exc)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — callback failure is non-fatal\n                pass\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# COMP-004 EvalRunner class (T03.05 / D-0049)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LogEvent:\n    \"\"\"Single JSONL line buffered by :class:`EvalRunner`.\n\n    The buffer holds events until ``home_path`` is known; once setup\n    completes the buffer is flushed to disk and subsequent events are\n    appended directly. Field shape matches the JSONL contract documented\n    in ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    event: str\n    ts_offset_sec: float\n    eval_id: str\n    step: str\n    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        # Build an ordered dict so JSON diff output stays stable; the\n        # round() pin is documented in D-0049/spec.md (\"Rounded to\n        # microseconds so JSON diff output stays compact and stable.\").\n        return {\n            \"event\": self.event,\n            \"ts_offset_sec\": round(self.ts_offset_sec, 6),\n            \"eval_id\": self.eval_id,\n            \"step\": self.step,\n            \"extra\": dict(self.extra),\n        }\n\n\nclass _JsonlLog:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe buffer + writer for the per-eval JSONL log.\n\n    The runner emits events from both the worker thread (every\n    lifecycle step except timeout teardown) and the main thread (the\n    ``timeout_fired`` + ``outcome`` events). A lock keeps the ordered\n    list of buffered events consistent and `write()` deterministic.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, eval_id: str, clock: Callable[[], float]) -> None:\n        self._eval_id = eval_id\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._start = clock()\n        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n        self._events: list[_LogEvent] = []\n\n    def _ts_offset(self) -> float:\n        return self._clock() - self._start\n\n    def emit(self, event: str, *, step: str, extra: Optional[Mapping[str, Any]] = None) -> None:\n        evt = _LogEvent(\n            event=event,\n            ts_offset_sec=self._ts_offset(),\n            eval_id=self._eval_id,\n            step=step,\n            extra=dict(extra or {}),\n        )\n        with self._lock:\n            self._events.append(evt)\n\n    def snapshot(self) -> tuple[_LogEvent, ...]:\n        with self._lock:\n            return tuple(self._events)\n\n    def write_to(self, path: Path) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the buffered events as one JSON object per line.\n\n        Failures are best-effort: the JSONL log is a forensic artifact,\n        not a load-bearing part of the outcome contract.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lock:\n            events = list(self._events)\n        try:\n            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            with path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                for evt in events:\n                    fh.write(json.dumps(evt.to_dict(), sort_keys=False))\n                    fh.write(\"\\n\")\n        except OSError:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort log write\n            return\n\n\nclass _LoggingHomeProxy:\n    \"\"\"Duck-typed proxy around ``HomeIsolation`` emitting setup/teardown events.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation`` is a frozen dataclass so we cannot subclass-and-\n    override its setup/teardown methods. Wrapping the instance keeps the\n    skeleton (``run_eval``) untouched while letting ``EvalRunner`` log\n    the four required events at the exact lifecycle boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, home: Any, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._home = home\n        self._log = log\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_STARTED, step=\"setup\")\n        try:\n            home_path = self._home.setup(config=config)\n        except BaseException:\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"setup\",\n            extra={\"home_path\": str(home_path)},\n        )\n        return home_path\n\n    def teardown(self, keep: bool) -> None:\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": keep},\n        )\n        try:\n            self._home.teardown(keep)\n        except BaseException as exc:\n            cls = type(exc)\n            self._log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED, step=\"teardown\")\n\n    def env(self) -> Mapping[str, str]:\n        return self._home.env()\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        return self._home.home_path\n\n\nclass _LoggingExecutor:\n    \"\"\"LifecycleExecutor proxy emitting spawn/inject/observe events.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, executor: LifecycleExecutor, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._log = log\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED, step=\"spawn\")\n        self._executor.spawn(ctx)\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_INJECT_STARTED, step=\"inject\")\n        self._executor.inject(ctx)\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> ObservedRun:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED, step=\"observe\")\n        observed = self._executor.observe(ctx)\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"observe\",\n            extra={\n                \"exit_code\": observed.exit_code,\n                \"duration_sec\": observed.duration_sec,\n            },\n        )\n        return observed\n\n\ndef _wrap_expect_with_log(\n    callable_: ExpectCallable, *, index: int, log: _JsonlLog\n) -> ExpectCallable:\n    \"\"\"Return a wrapped ExpectCallable that emits assertion events.\n\n    The wrapper preserves the original signature so ``run_eval`` cannot\n    tell it is being observed. The original callable's ``__name__`` is\n    captured into the ``assertion_started`` / ``assertion_completed``\n    event payloads so post-mortem tooling can correlate JSONL rows with\n    DSL primitives.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name = getattr(callable_, \"__name__\", None)\n    if name == \"<lambda>\":\n        name = None\n\n    def _logging_expect(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name},\n        )\n        result = callable_(ctx)\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name, \"passed\": bool(result.passed)},\n        )\n        return result\n\n    # Preserve attributes so frameworks that introspect __name__ still work.\n    _logging_expect.__wrapped__ = callable_  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    if name is not None:\n        _logging_expect.__name__ = name\n    return _logging_expect\n\n\nclass EvalRunner:\n    \"\"\"COMP-004 per-eval runner with JSONL logging + timeout enforcement.\n\n    Wraps the FR-LC1 lifecycle skeleton (``run_eval``) in a class that\n    owns two cross-cutting concerns the skeleton intentionally deferred:\n\n    1. A per-eval JSONL log under\n       ``home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`` covering every\n       lifecycle transition.\n    2. Per-eval timeout enforcement — when\n       ``EvalSpec.timeout_sec`` is set, the runner returns an\n       ``EvalOutcome`` with status ``TIMEOUT`` if the lifecycle does not\n       complete within budget.\n\n    See ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md`` for the full contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    LOG_DIR_RELPATH: ClassVar[Path] = Path(\".eval-logs\")\n\n    # NFR-REL2 (T03.08 / D-0051): the bounded retry policy. Failed evals\n    # are *not* retried automatically; the user re-runs them with\n    # ``superclaude eval run --eval <id>`` after diagnosing. The\n    # constants below pin the contract so future R3-mit work (T05.23)\n    # has a single place to extend: tags carrying ``MCP_FLAKY_TAG`` will\n    # be eligible for one in-process retry once OQ-10 closes. Until\n    # then, ``retry_count`` is required to be exactly\n    # ``DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT`` and any non-zero value is rejected at\n    # construction time. See ``docs/eval/retry.md`` for the user-facing\n    # subset re-run guidance.\n    DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT: ClassVar[int] = 0\n    MCP_FLAKY_TAG: ClassVar[str] = \"MCP-flaky\"\n\n    EVENT_SETUP_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_started\"\n    EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_completed\"\n    EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED: ClassVar[str] = \"hooks_deployed\"\n    EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"spawn_started\"\n    EVENT_INJECT_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"inject_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_completed\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_started\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_started\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_error\"\n    EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"timeout_fired\"\n    EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"interrupted_fired\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_started\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_completed\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_error\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_skipped\"\n    EVENT_OUTCOME: ClassVar[str] = \"outcome\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        home: HomeIsolation,\n        config: EvalConfig,\n        executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n        deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n        keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n        default_timeout_sec: Optional[float] = None,\n        clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n        retry_count: int = DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT,\n    ) -> None:\n        # NFR-REL2 (T03.08): the harness produces a deterministic\n        # single-pass run. ``retry_count`` is accepted as a constructor\n        # parameter so callers can document intent, but until R3-mit\n        # (T05.23) lands the MCP-flaky retry-once branch the only\n        # accepted value is the default. Reject anything else loudly so\n        # an accidental sweep does not silently re-execute evals and\n        # break the orchestrator's N'-vs-K invariant (FR-RPT1 / T03.11).\n        if retry_count != self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalRunner retry_count must be {self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT} \"\n                f\"(NFR-REL2 bounded retry policy); got {retry_count!r}. \"\n                \"Re-run failing evals via 'superclaude eval run --eval <id>' \"\n                \"instead — see docs/eval/retry.md.\"\n            )\n\n        self._home = home\n        self._config = config\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._run_dir = run_dir\n        self._artifacts_dir = artifacts_dir\n        self._stdout_path = stdout_path\n        self._stderr_path = stderr_path\n        self._transcript_path = transcript_path\n        self._expect_callables = tuple(expect_callables)\n        self._deploy_hooks = deploy_hooks\n        self._keep_home_on_pass = keep_home_on_pass\n        self._default_timeout_sec = default_timeout_sec\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n        self._retry_count = retry_count\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Execute the per-eval lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n        Wraps :func:`run_eval` with logging proxies + a per-eval timeout\n        budget. The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n        branch this class adds.\n\n        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n\n        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. If the signal handler has\n        # already flipped the token before we even start the worker,\n        # return INTERRUPTED immediately so the orchestrator does not\n        # waste a HomeIsolation setup on an eval whose result will be\n        # discarded anyway.\n        if self._cancellation_token is not None and self._cancellation_token.is_cancelled():\n            return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n\n        # Holders for the worker thread. ``outcome_holder`` is populated\n        # on clean return; ``exc_holder`` is populated when run_eval\n        # raised (KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit, mainly — every other\n        # exception is folded into an ERRORED outcome by run_eval\n        # itself).\n        outcome_holder: dict[str, EvalOutcome] = {}\n        exc_holder: dict[str, BaseException] = {}\n\n        logged_home = _LoggingHomeProxy(self._home, log)\n        logged_executor = _LoggingExecutor(self._executor, log)\n        wrapped_expects = tuple(\n            _wrap_expect_with_log(c, index=i, log=log)\n            for i, c in enumerate(self._expect_callables)\n        )\n\n        # The wrapped ``deploy_hooks`` emits ``hooks_deployed`` once the\n        # underlying call returns; the spec does not require this event\n        # but it is the natural place to record that step 2 completed.\n        original_deploy = self._deploy_hooks\n\n        def logged_deploy(home_path: Path) -> None:\n            original_deploy(home_path)\n            log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED, step=\"deploy_hooks\")\n\n        # ``on_teardown_error`` is invoked by ``_safe_teardown`` when\n        # teardown raises. The proxy already logs the failure via\n        # ``EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR``; the callback is a no-op so the\n        # caller-injected behaviour (preserved by FR-LC1) keeps working\n        # if a future caller wires one through ``EvalRunner``.\n        def on_teardown_error(_exc: BaseException) -> None:\n            return None\n\n        def worker() -> None:\n            try:\n                outcome_holder[\"result\"] = run_eval(\n                    spec,\n                    home=logged_home,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    config=self._config,\n                    run_dir=self._run_dir,\n                    artifacts_dir=self._artifacts_dir,\n                    stdout_path=self._stdout_path,\n                    stderr_path=self._stderr_path,\n                    transcript_path=self._transcript_path,\n                    executor=logged_executor,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    expect_callables=wrapped_expects,\n                    deploy_hooks=logged_deploy,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=self._keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for re-raise\n                exc_holder[\"exc\"] = exc\n\n        # ``daemon=True`` so a hung worker cannot block process exit.\n        # NFR-REL1 (T03.07) wires the PtyDriver kill + zombie reap that\n        # makes a hung worker rare in production.\n        thread = threading.Thread(\n            target=worker,\n            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n            daemon=True,\n        )\n        thread.start()\n        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n\n        if thread.is_alive():\n            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n\n        # Worker returned. Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. Flush the JSONL log first so\n        # the partial events survive the re-raise.\n        if \"exc\" in exc_holder:\n            exc = exc_holder[\"exc\"]\n            # NFR-REL1 (T03.07): when a cancellation token is wired, the\n            # runner converts a worker-thread KeyboardInterrupt /\n            # SystemExit directly into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome so the\n            # orchestrator's ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed loop never\n            # has to translate an exception into a status. Without a\n            # token, propagation is preserved verbatim so the FR-LC1\n            # contract (and the existing ``test_runner_class.py`` AC4\n            # case) stays unchanged.\n            if (\n                self._cancellation_token is not None\n                and isinstance(exc, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))\n            ):\n                self._cancellation_token.cancel()\n                return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n            self._flush_log(log)\n            raise exc\n\n        outcome = outcome_holder[\"result\"]\n        # Record the terminal classification so post-mortem tooling can\n        # tell whether the run ended cleanly or was reclassified.\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _resolve_timeout(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> Optional[float]:\n        \"\"\"Return the resolved per-eval timeout, or ``None`` for unbounded.\n\n        ``spec.timeout_sec`` takes precedence; if absent or 0 the\n        runner falls back to ``default_timeout_sec``. ``0`` is treated\n        as \"use the default\" rather than \"no timeout\" because a literal\n        zero would always fire and is never useful in practice.\n        \"\"\"\n        spec_timeout = spec.timeout_sec\n        if spec_timeout is not None and spec_timeout > 0:\n            return float(spec_timeout)\n        if self._default_timeout_sec is not None and self._default_timeout_sec > 0:\n            return float(self._default_timeout_sec)\n        return None\n\n    def _handle_timeout(\n        self,\n        *,\n        spec: EvalSpec,\n        log: _JsonlLog,\n        timeout_sec: Optional[float],\n    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Emit timeout events, attempt best-effort teardown, return outcome.\n\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) extends the T03.05 timeout path with a\n        subprocess kill + zombie reap step: if the wired executor\n        exposes a ``cancel()`` method (the production\n        ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver`` does), the runner calls\n        it from the main thread so the PtyDriver child terminates and\n        ``wait()`` reaps the zombie before the orchestrator collects\n        the next outcome. Failure is swallowed because the TIMEOUT\n        outcome must always return cleanly; the JSONL log records the\n        cancel attempt.\n        \"\"\"\n        budget = float(timeout_sec) if timeout_sec is not None else 0.0\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED,\n            step=\"timeout\",\n            extra={\"timeout_sec\": budget},\n        )\n\n        # NFR-REL1: kill the executor's subprocess + reap zombie before\n        # we attempt teardown. Documented on D-0050/spec.md.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"timeout\")\n\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": True},\n        )\n        try:\n            # The worker may still hold references to the HomeIsolation\n            # instance; HomeIsolation.teardown is idempotent enough that\n            # a main-thread call is safe even if the worker is mid-step.\n            # Failures are swallowed so the TIMEOUT outcome always\n            # returns cleanly.\n            if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort\n            cls = type(exc)\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n\n        outcome = EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"TIMEOUT\",\n            duration_sec=budget,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\",\n        )\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    def _cancel_executor(self, *, log: _JsonlLog, reason: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Best-effort PtyDriver kill + zombie reap on the wired executor.\n\n        The production executor (``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` driving a\n        ``PtyDriver``) exposes a ``cancel()`` method that calls\n        ``PtyDriver.terminate(force=True)`` followed by ``close()`` so\n        the child process is signalled and ``wait()`` reaps the\n        resulting zombie. Test stubs that do not need cancellation\n        simply omit the method — ``getattr`` returns ``None`` and the\n        runner skips the step.\n\n        Failures are swallowed so a flaky cancel cannot block a\n        TIMEOUT / INTERRUPTED outcome from returning. The JSONL log\n        records ``executor_cancel_*`` events under the ``timeout``\n        step so post-mortem tooling can confirm the kill attempt\n        landed.\n        \"\"\"\n        cancel = getattr(self._executor, \"cancel\", None)\n        if cancel is None or not callable(cancel):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED,\n                step=reason,\n                extra={\"reason\": \"executor has no cancel() method\"},\n            )\n            return\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED,\n            step=reason,\n            extra={},\n        )\n        try:\n            cancel()\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort kill\n            cls = type(exc)\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR,\n                step=reason,\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            return\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED,\n            step=reason,\n            extra={},\n        )\n\n    def _make_interrupted_outcome(\n        self, *, spec: EvalSpec, log: _JsonlLog\n    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome and flush the JSONL log.\n\n        Used when:\n\n        * The cancellation token was already set before\n          ``EvalRunner.run`` started (skip the lifecycle entirely).\n        * The worker raised ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` and\n          a cancellation token is wired (NFR-REL1 cooperative path).\n\n        ``duration_sec`` is pinned to ``0.0`` because no observable\n        portion of the lifecycle ran; the Reporter renders the column\n        verbatim so a stale value would confuse downstream readers.\n        \"\"\"\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED,\n            step=\"interrupt\",\n            extra={},\n        )\n        # Best-effort cancel: if the executor was mid-flight when the\n        # interrupt arrived, kill its subprocess + reap zombie.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"interrupt\")\n        # Best-effort teardown so a partial HOME (forensic artefact) is\n        # preserved when isolation already ran.\n        if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\"keep\": True},\n            )\n            try:\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n            except BaseException as exc:  # The JSONL log is a forensic artifact, never\n        load-bearing for outcome classification.\n        \"\"\"\n        home_path = self._resolve_home_path()\n        if home_path is None:\n            return\n        # ``eval_id`` is regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 upstream so it is safe\n        # to use verbatim in a filename. We still take the first event\n        # for the id to avoid trusting any later mutation.\n        snapshot = log.snapshot()\n        if not snapshot:\n            return\n        eval_id = snapshot[0].eval_id\n        log_path = home_path / self.LOG_DIR_RELPATH / f\"{eval_id}.jsonl\"\n        log.write_to(log_path)\n\n    def _resolve_home_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME path if available, ``None`` otherwise.\n\n        ``HomeIsolation.home_path`` raises when setup has not run; we\n        catch that and return ``None`` so a setup-time failure never\n        promotes itself into a runner exception.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            return Path(self._home.home_path)\n        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — home_path may raise pre-setup\n            return None\n","numLines":1186,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1186}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YBCrfikMdjLEMNUfwKorZP","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-003 RunOrchestrator — parallel eval scheduler.\n2\t\n3\tTask T03.15 / Deliverable D-0057 / Roadmap row R-057 (COMP-003).\n4\t\n5\tThe orchestrator is the single component that turns a sequence of\n6\texpanded :class:`EvalSpec` rows into a sequence of :class:`EvalOutcome`\n7\trows by scheduling per-spec :class:`EvalRunner` workers through\n8\t``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed`` — the AC6\n9\tpattern the design-spec pins for cliEval. The implementation deliberately\n10\tmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\n11\tso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\n12\tthe same scheduling skeleton.\n13\t\n14\tResponsibilities\n15\t================\n16\t\n17\tThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\n18\tintentionally defers:\n19\t\n20\t1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n21\t   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n22\t   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n23\t   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n24\t   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n25\t   effective value.\n26\t2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n27\t   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n28\t   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n29\t   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n30\t   spec.\n31\t3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n32\t   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n33\t   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n34\t   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n35\t   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n36\t   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n37\t   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n38\t   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n39\t   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n40\t   into a status.\n41\t\n42\tThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\n43\tinstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\n44\tallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n45\t:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\n46\trunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n47\t\n48\tWorker contract\n49\t===============\n50\t\n51\tThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\n52\titself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\n53\tisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\n54\tsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\n55\tcaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\n56\tthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n57\t:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\n58\ttypically write::\n59\t\n60\t    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n61\t        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n62\t        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n63\t        return runner.run(spec)\n64\t\n65\tThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\n66\tmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\n67\ta canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n68\t\n69\tIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\n70\tof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\n71\tthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\n72\trecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n73\t:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\n74\tthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n75\t\"\"\"\n76\t\n77\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n78\t\n79\tfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\n80\tfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n81\t\n82\tfrom .disk_budget import DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON, DiskBudgetPoller\n83\tfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\n84\tfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n85\t\n86\t__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n90\t# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. The implementation deliberately\nmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\nso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\nthe same scheduling skeleton.\n\nResponsibilities\n================\n\nThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\nintentionally defers:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n   effective value.\n2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n   spec.\n3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n   into a status.\n\nThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\ninstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\nallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\nrunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n\nWorker contract\n===============\n\nThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\nitself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\nisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\nsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\ncaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\nthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\ntypically write::\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n        return runner.run(spec)\n\nThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\nmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\na canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n\nIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\nof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\nthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\nrecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\nthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n\nfrom .disk_budget import DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON, DiskBudgetPoller\nfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n\n__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n\n\n# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Production wiring composes\n# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\nEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n\n\nclass RunOrchestrator:\n    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    run_one:\n        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n        out of :meth:`run`.\n    cancellation_token:\n        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    disk_budget_poller:\n        Optional :class:`DiskBudgetPoller` enforcing the NFR-PERF4\n        ``--max-disk-mb`` budget (T03.19 / D-0060 / R-060). When wired,\n        the orchestrator starts the poller before submission, checks\n        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.is_breached` alongside the cancellation\n        token between submissions, and synthesises a ``SKIPPED`` outcome\n        with ``skip_reason=\"disk_budget_exceeded\"`` for every spec that\n        was never scheduled after a breach. In-flight workers run to\n        completion — the poller never interrupts a thread. A disabled\n        poller (``max_disk_mb=0``) is silently no-op'd by\n        :meth:`DiskBudgetPoller.start`, so the orchestrator can be wired\n        with a poller unconditionally.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n        disk_budget_poller: Optional[DiskBudgetPoller] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n        self._disk_budget_poller = disk_budget_poller\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the\n        # disk-budget poller flipped its breach flag. They receive a\n        # synthesised ``SKIPPED`` outcome with ``skip_reason ==\n        # \"disk_budget_exceeded\"`` so the Reporter can distinguish a\n        # disk-budget abort from a SIGINT.\n        disk_budget_skipped_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        # The poller is started before the submission loop so it has at\n        # least one tick of head-start. It is shut down in a ``finally``\n        # so an unhandled exception in the pool body still releases the\n        # background thread.\n        poller = self._disk_budget_poller\n        if poller is not None:\n            poller.start()\n        try:\n            with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n                max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n            ) as pool:\n                futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n                for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                    # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                    # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                    # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                    # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                    # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                    # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                    #\n                    # Cancellation is checked *before* the disk-budget\n                    # breach so a SIGINT received concurrently with a\n                    # breach routes the spec to INTERRUPTED (operator\n                    # intent dominates a resource limit).\n                    if self._is_cancelled():\n                        cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    if self._is_disk_budget_exceeded():\n                        disk_budget_skipped_indices.append(index)\n                        continue\n                    future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                    futures[future] = index\n\n                # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n                # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n                # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n                # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n                # In-flight workers continue to completion even after a\n                # disk-budget breach — the AC6 contract requires \"in-\n                # flight evals complete\" before the run exits.\n                for future in as_completed(futures):\n                    index = futures[future]\n                    spec = specs[index]\n                    try:\n                        outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                        # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                        # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                        # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                        # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                        # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                        # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                        outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n        finally:\n            if poller is not None:\n                poller.stop()\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n        # Backfill SKIPPED outcomes for specs we declined to submit\n        # because the disk budget was breached. Same post-shutdown\n        # placement as the INTERRUPTED backfill — once we are here the\n        # pool has drained, so the synthesised outcomes cannot race\n        # with an in-flight result.\n        for index in disk_budget_skipped_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._disk_budget_skipped_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. The cast filters the\n        # ``Optional[...]`` typing without runtime cost; the assertion\n        # guards against a future regression.\n        assert all(o is not None for o in outcomes), (\n            \"RunOrchestrator: one or more outcome slots were left empty\"\n        )\n        return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _invoke_worker(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Indirection so subclasses / tests can hook the call site.\"\"\"\n        return self._run_one(spec)\n\n    def _is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        token = self._cancellation_token\n        return token is not None and token.is_cancelled()\n\n    def _is_disk_budget_exceeded(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"``True`` when the wired poller has flagged a budget breach.\n\n        Returns ``False`` when no poller was wired or the poller is\n        still healthy. Mirrors :meth:`_is_cancelled` so the submission\n        loop reads naturally.\n        \"\"\"\n        poller = self._disk_budget_poller\n        return poller is not None and poller.is_breached()\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _disk_budget_skipped_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build a SKIPPED outcome for a spec that lost out to the disk budget.\n\n        ``skip_reason`` carries the stable\n        :data:`disk_budget.DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON` token so the\n        Reporter can match on the constant without parsing free-form\n        text. ``skip_flag_triggered`` records the CLI flag operators\n        toggle to relax the budget, mirroring the convention used by\n        capability-gate skips elsewhere in the harness.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"SKIPPED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_REASON,\n            skip_flag_triggered=\"--max-disk-mb\",\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _interrupted_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build the synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for a skipped spec.\n\n        Mirrors :meth:`EvalRunner._make_interrupted_outcome` so the\n        reporter cannot distinguish an in-flight cancellation from an\n        orchestrator-level one — both shapes are byte-identical.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _errored_outcome(spec: EvalSpec, exc: BaseException) -> EvalOutcome:\n Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n","numLines":374,"startLine":1,"totalLines":374}}}

---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n190\t# NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck (T03.17 / D-0059)\n191\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n192\t#\n193\t# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n194\t# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n195\t# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n196\t# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n197\t# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n198\t# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n199\t# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n200\t# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n201\t\n202\tRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n203\t\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n204\t\n205\tThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\n206\tthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\n207\tconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n208\t\"\"\"\n209\t\n210\tRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n211\t\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n212\t\n213\t``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\n214\tthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n215\t\"\"\"\n216\t\n217\tRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n218\t\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n219\t\n220\tThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\n221\tit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n222\t\"\"\"\n223\t\n224\tPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n225\t\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n226\t\n227\tMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\n228\tbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\n229\tthan emit a misleading number.\n230\t\"\"\"\n231\t\n232\t\n233\tdef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n234\t    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n235\t\n236\t    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n237\t    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n238\t    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n239\t    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n240\t    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n241\t    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n242\t    \"\"\"\n243\t\n244\t    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n245\t    if not meminfo.is_file():\n246\t        return None\n247\t    try:\n248\t        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n249\t    except OSError:\n250\t        return None\n251\t    available: Optional[int] = None\n252\t    free: Optional[int] = None\n253\t    for line in text.splitlines():\n254\t        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n255\t        key = key.strip()\n256\t        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n257\t            continue\n258\t        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n259\t        if not tokens:\n260\t            continue\n261\t        try:\n262\t            kb = int(tokens[0])\n263\t        except ValueError:\n264\t            continue\n265\t        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n266\t        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n267\t        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n268\t        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n269\t        value = kb * multiplier\n270\t        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n271\t            available = value\n272\t        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n273\t            free = value\n274\t    if available is not None:\n275\t        return available\n276\t    return free\n277\t\n278\t\n279\tdef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n280\t    *,\n281\t    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n282\t    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n283\t    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n284\t    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n285\t) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n286\t    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n287\t\n288\t    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n289\t    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n294\t    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n295\t\n296\t    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n297\t try:\n314\t        free_ram = probe()\n315\t    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n316\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n317\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n318\t            passed=False,\n319\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n320\t            description=description,\n321\t            detail=(\n322\t                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n323\t                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n324\t            ),\n325\t        )\n326\t    if free_ram is None:\n327\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n328\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n329\t            passed=False,\n330\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n331\t            description=description,\n332\t            detail=(\n333\t                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n334\t                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n335\t            ),\n336\t        )\n337\t    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n338\t    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n339\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n340\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n341\t            passed=False,\n342\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n343\t            description=description,\n344\t            detail=(\n345\t                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n346\t                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n347\t            ),\n348\t        )\n349\t    return The loader is injectable\n603\t    for tests that want to stub schema/regex/capability gates without\n604\t    touching the file system.\n605\t    \"\"\"\n606\t\n607\t    loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n608\t    summaries: list[SuiteSummary] = []\n609\t    for manifest in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n610\t        parsed: ParsedSuite = loader.load(manifest)\n611\t        summaries.append(\n612\t            SuiteSummary(\n613\t                name=parsed.name,\n614\t                version=parsed.version,\n615\t                eval_count=len(parsed.evals),\n616\t                source=manifest,\n617\t            )\n618\t        )\n619\t    return summaries\n620\t\n621\t\n622\tdef render_list_text(summaries: Iterable[SuiteSummary]) -> str:\n623\t    \"\"\"Render the human-readable ``eval list`` output.\"\"\"\n624\t\n625\t    rows = list(summaries)\n626\t    lines = [\"superclaude eval list:\"]\n627\t    if not rows:\n628\t        lines.append(\"  (no suites found)\")\n629\t        return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n630\t    for row in rows:\n631\t        lines.append(\n632\t            f\"  - for a\n706\t      scratch-root violation it quotes :data:`SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY`\n707\t      verbatim so operators can correct the invocation without\n708\t      consulting the docs.\n709\t    \"\"\"\n710\t    if output_dir is not None:\n711\t        try:\n712\t            resolve_scratch_root(output_dir)\n713\t        except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n714\t            click.echo(\n715\t                f\"eval doctor: {format_scratch_root_violation(exc)}\",\n716\t                err=True,\n717\t            )\n718\t            sys.exit(SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE)\n719\t\n720\t    skip_flags: tuple[str, ...] = (\"--no-mcp\",) if no_mcp else ()\n721\t    report = build_doctor_report(\n722\t        skip_flags=skip_flags,\n723\t        requested_parallel=parallel,\n724\t    )\n725\t\n726\t    if as_json:\n727\t        payload = doctor_payload(report, check_coverage=check_coverage)\n728\t        click.echo(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True))\n729\t    else:\n730\t        click.echo(render_checklist(report))\n731\t        if check_coverage:\n732\t            click.echo(\"coverage gate: deferred to M4 T04.14\")\n733\t\n734\t    # NFR-PERF2 SOFT-SKIP warning: when ``--parallel >= 15`` was requested\n735\t    # and the host could not certify the free-RAM budget, emit a stderr\n736\t    # warning containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token. (known: {known})\"\n863\t        )\n864\t\n865\t\n866\tdef _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n867\t    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n868\t\n869\t    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n870\t    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n871\t    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n872\t    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n873\t    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n874\t    eval that did suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n921\t    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n922\t\n923\t    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n924\t\n925\t    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n926\t    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n927\t       convention used by the built-in suites).\n928\t    3. Broken manifests\n930\t       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n931\t       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n932\t\n933\t    Raises:\n934\t        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"HARD check: ``claude --version`` parses and meets the minimum.\n\n    The version floor (R1-mit / T02.20) is sourced from ``EvalConfig`` —\n    there is no in-doctor hard-coded copy. whose default tracks\n    :data:`DEFAULT_MIN_CLAUDE_VERSION`.\n    \"\"\"\n    probe = probe or _default_claude_version_probe\n    if min_version is None:\n        active_config = config if config is not None else EvalConfig()\n        min_version = active_config.min_claude_version\n    description = f\"Claude CLI >= {_format_version(min_version)}\"\n    try:\n        raw = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - probe is a user-supplied callable\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=f\"version probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\",\n        )\n    if not raw:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=\"claude --version not callable\",\n        )\n    parsed = _parse_version(raw)\n    if parsed is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=f\"could not parse version from {raw!r}\",\n        )\n    passed = parsed >= min_version\n    detail = f\"claude
