passed=passed,\n        failure_mode=\"hard\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=detail,\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_claude_home(home: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"HARD check: ``~/.claude/`` (or override) exists and is a directory.\"\"\"\n    target = home if home is not None else Path.home() / \".claude\"\n    exists = target.is_dir()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"filesystem.claude_home\",\n        passed=exists,\n        failure_mode=\"hard\",\n        description=\"~/.claude/ directory exists\",\n        detail=str(target) if exists else f\"{target} not found\",\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck (T03.17 / D-0059)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n\nRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n\nThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\nthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\nconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n\n``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\nthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n\nThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\nit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n\"\"\"\n\nPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n\nMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\nbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\nthan emit a misleading number.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n\n    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n\n    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n\n    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n\n    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n      mode without parsing structure.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n            ),\n        )\n    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring lands at M2; the row stays SOFT-SKIP so\n    doctor still exits 0 on a clean dev machine until the upstream task\n    materialises ``cli/eval/pty/__init__.py``.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = pty_dir if pty_dir is not None else Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"pty\"\n    init_file = target / \"__init__.py\"\n    passed = init_file.is_file()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"vendored.ptytest\",\n        passed=passed,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=\"ptytest vendored under cli/eval/pty/\",\n        detail=str(target) if passed else f\"{init_file} not found (vendored at M2)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _extend_report(\n    base: CapabilityReport,\n    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n\n    The base report's ``blocked_evals`` and ``skip_flags`` are preserved\n    verbatim — supplementary doctor rows only contribute to the three\n    failure buckets (``hard_failures``, ``soft_skips``, ``soft_xfails``).\n    \"\"\"\n    rows = base.report + extras\n    hard_failures = list(base.hard_failures)\n    soft_skips = list(base.soft_skips)\n    soft_xfails = list(base.soft_xfails)\n    for status in extras:\n        if status.passed:\n            continue\n        if status.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n            hard_failures.append(status.name)\n        elif status.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n            soft_skips.append(status.name)\n        else:  # \"xfail\"\n            soft_xfails.append(status.name)\n    return replace(\n        base,\n        report=rows,\n        hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n    *,\n    gates: Optional[CapabilityGates] = None,\n    claude_version_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[str]]] = None,\n    claude_home: Optional[Path] = None,\n    pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None,\n    config: Optional[EvalConfig] = None,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int] = None,\n    free_ram_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Aggregate :class:`CapabilityGates` + doctor-specific checks.\n\n    Args:\n :class:`CapabilityGates` (test hook).\n        claude_version_probe: Override for ``claude --version`` invocation.\n        claude_home: Override for ``~/.claude/`` path.\n        pty_dir: Override tuple[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(glyph, tag)`` for the human checklist line.\"\"\"\n    if row.passed:\n        return \"[ok]\", \"\"\n    if row.skipped_by_flag:\n        return \"[--]\", \" \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n '.join(report.soft_skips)}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\ndef render_hard_failure_artifact(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the stderr HARD-failure artifact per FR-CLI4 AC.\"\"\"\n    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n    for name in report.hard_failures:\n        row = by_name.get(name)\n        if row doctor_payload(report: CapabilityReport, *, check_coverage: bool) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Build the ``--json`` payload.\n\n    Extends :meth:`CapabilityReport.to_json` with a ``coverage_gate``\n    marker \"task\": \"T04.14\",\n    }\n    return payload\n\n\nHARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE = 2\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FR-CLI2 ``eval list`` (T01.21 / D-0018)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR: Path = SCHEMA_PATH.parent\n\"\"\"Default location for built-in suite manifests.\n\nResolves to ``src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/``. The directory currently\nonly ships ``suite.schema.json`` and an ``__init__.py``; built-in\n``*.yaml`` manifests will land in subsequent milestones. The glob in\n:func:`discover_suite_manifests` filters to ``*.yaml`` so the schema and\npackage marker never appear in the listing.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``suites_dir/*.yaml`` sorted by filename for determinism.\n\n    ``eval list`` MUST produce identical output for a given suite\n    directory across invocations (FR-CLI2 AC), so the sort is part of the\n    contract — not an implementation detail.\n\n    A missing or non-directory ``suites_dir`` is treated as the\n    empty-directory case (no suites found) rather than an error: a fresh\n    checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not break\n    ``eval list``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not suites_dir.is_dir():\n        return []\n    return sorted(suites_dir.glob(\"*.yaml\"))\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass SuiteSummary:\n    \"\"\"One row in the ``eval list`` output.\n\n    Attributes:\n        name: Manifest ``name`` field (verbatim).\n        version: Manifest ``version`` field (verbatim, string).\n        eval_count: Number of evals after parameterize expansion. This\n            reflects \"how many evals would run\" rather than the raw\n            ``evals[]`` length, which matches operator intent.\n        source: Filesystem path of the manifest (used for human output\n            and stable JSON ordering).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    eval_count: int\n    source: Path\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict:\n        return {\n            \"name\": self.name,\n            \"version\": self.version,\n            \"eval_count\": self.eval_count,\n        }\n\n\ndef summarize_suites(\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    *,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> list[SuiteSummary]:\n    \"\"\"Load every ``*.yaml`` in ``suites_dir`` and project to summaries.\n\n    Any :class:`SuiteLoaderError` raised by :class:`SuiteLoader` propagates\n    so the CLI boundary can map it to\n    :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). When >= 15 the \"\n        \"doctor runs the NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck and warns on hosts \"\n        f\"with less than {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} free.\"\n    ),\n)\ndef doctor(\n    as_json: bool,\n    no_mcp: bool,\n    check_coverage: bool,\n    output_dir: Optional[Path],\n    parallel: Optional[int],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Verify host preconditions for ``superclaude eval run``.\n\n    Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — every HARD capability satisfied.\n    * ``2`` — at least one HARD capability failed, or ``--output-dir``\n      was supplied and escaped the OPS-002 / AC12 scratch-root policy.\n      In both cases the stderr artifact identifies the cause; for a\n      scratch-root violation it quotes :data:`SCRATCH_ROOT_POLICY`\n      verbatim so operators can correct the invocation without\n      consulting the docs.\n    \"\"\"\n    if output_dir is not None:\n        try:\n            resolve_scratch_root(output_dir)\n        except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n            click.echo(\n                f\"eval doctor: {format_scratch_root_violation(exc)}\",\n                err=True,\n            )\n            sys.exit(SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    skip_flags: tuple[str, ...] = (\"--no-mcp\",) if no_mcp else ()\n    report = build_doctor_report(\n        skip_flags=skip_flags,\n        requested_parallel=parallel,\n    )\n\n    if as_json:\n        payload = doctor_payload(report, check_coverage=check_coverage)\n        click.echo(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True))\n    else:\n        click.echo(render_checklist(report))\n        if check_coverage:\n            click.echo(\"coverage gate: deferred to M4 T04.14\")\n\n    # NFR-PERF2 SOFT-SKIP warning: when ``--parallel >= 15`` was requested\n    # and the host could not certify the free-RAM budget, emit a stderr\n    # warning containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token. The\n    # warning fires regardless of ``--json`` so CI logs and machine\n    # consumers see the same signal — the JSON payload still carries the\n    # row under ``soft_skips`` for structured parsing.\n    ram_row = next(\n        (r for r in report.report if r.name == \"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\"),\n        None,\n    )\n    if ram_row is not None and not ram_row.passed:\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval doctor: NFR-PERF2 warning: {ram_row.detail}\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n\n    if report.hard_failures:\n        click.echo(render_hard_failure_artifact(report), err=True)\n        sys.exit(HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE)\n\n\n@eval_group.command(\"list\")\n@click.option(\n    \"--json\",\n    \"as_json\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Emit a JSON array of {name, version, eval_count} entries.\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--suites-dir\",\n    \"suites_dir\",\n    type=click.Path(file_okay=False, path_type=Path),\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Override the suite manifest directory. Defaults to \"\n        \"src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/.\"\n    ),\n)\ndef eval_list(as_json: bool, suites_dir: Optional[Path]) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Enumerate suite manifests under ``cli/eval/suites/``.\n\n    Each row reports the manifest ``name``, ``version`` field, and the\n    post-parameterize-expansion eval count. An empty or missing suites\n    directory prints ``(no suites found)`` and still exits ``0``.\n\n    Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — every discovered manifest loaded green (or no manifests\n      found).\n    * ``2`` (``SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE``) — at least one manifest\n      failed schema validation, eval-id regex, or capability resolution;\n      the stderr line names the offending manifest and the typed error\n      class so operators can locate the failure.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    target = suites_dir if suites_dir is not None else _DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR\n    try:\n        summaries = summarize_suites(target)\n    except SuiteLoaderError as exc:  # type: ignore[misc]\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval list: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n        sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    if as_json:\n        click.echo(json.dumps(list_payload(summaries), indent=2, sort_keys=True))\n    else:\n        click.echo(render_list_text(summaries))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FR-CLI3 ``eval describe`` (T01.22 / D-0019)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\nSUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`SuiteNotFound` reaches the CLI.\n\nMirrors :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` so operators see a single\n\"harness rejected the describe request\" outcome regardless of which gate\nfired (schema, id regex, capability, or missing suite name).\n\"\"\"\n\n\nEVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`EvalNotFound` reaches the CLI.\n\nFR-CLI3 AC: ``--eval <id>`` with no matching expanded eval id exits 2\nwith the typed ``EvalNotFound`` class name on stderr.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass SuiteNotFound(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when ``--suite <name>`` resolves to no manifest on disk.\n\n    Carries the requested ``suite`` token and the ``suites_dir`` that was\n    searched so the stderr line names both. CLI callers MUST map this to\n    :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> None:\n        self.suite = suite\n        self.suites_dir = suites_dir\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"no manifest matched --suite {suite!r} in {suites_dir}\"\n        )\n\n\nclass EvalNotFound(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when ``--eval <id>`` does not match a post-expansion eval id.\n\n    Carries the requested ``eval_id`` plus the suite ``name`` and the\n    sorted tuple of known ids so the stderr line is forensic. CLI callers\n    MUST map this to :data:`EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        eval_id: str,\n        suite_name: str,\n        known_ids: Iterable[str],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.suite_name = suite_name\n        self.known_ids: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(known_ids)\n        known = \", \".join(self.known_ids) if self.known_ids else \"<none>\"\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval {eval_id!r} not in suite {suite_name!r} (known: {known})\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"id\": spec.id,\n        \"title\": spec.title,\n    }\n    if spec.category:\n        payload[\"category\"] = spec.category\n    if spec.requires:\n        payload[\"requires\"] = list(spec.requires)\n    if spec.timeout_sec is not None:\n        payload[\"timeout_sec\"] = spec.timeout_sec\n    if spec.isolation is not None:\n        payload[\"isolation\"] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    return payload\n\n\ndef _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed: ParsedSuite) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n\n    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n    \"what would run if you invoked ``eval run``\" view per FR-CLI3 AC).\n    Suite-level fields are verbatim from the manifest so the output\n    mirrors the validated source byte-for-byte modulo the ``evals``\n    expansion.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return {\n        \"name\": parsed.name,\n        \"version\": parsed.version,\n        \"description\": parsed.description,\n        \"defaults\": dict(parsed.defaults),\n        \"required_binaries\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.required_binaries],\n        \"optional_capabilities\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.optional_capabilities],\n        \"evals\": [_evalspec_to_dict(spec) for spec in parsed.evals],\n    }\n\n\ndef resolve_suite_manifest(suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. See\n            :func:`resolve_suite_manifest` for resolution precedence.\n        suites_dir: Directory to search when ``suite`` is not a direct\n            path. Tests inject a tmp dir via ``--suites-dir``.\n        eval_id: Optional post-expansion eval id (``E1``, ``E2.1``) to\n            filter the output to a single eval. Missing → ``EvalNotFound``.\n        loader: Injectable :class:`SuiteLoader` for tests that need to\n            stub capability resolution.\n\n    Returns:\n        A dict suitable for ``yaml.safe_dump`` / ``json.dumps``. When\n        ``eval_id`` is ``None``, the dict is the full manifest envelope;\n        when ``eval_id`` is set, the dict is a single ``evalEntry`` shape\n        (the same projection :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` produces).\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: Resolution rule chain produced no manifest.\n        EvalNotFound: ``eval_id`` did not match any post-expansion id.\n        SchemaError / InvalidEvalId / UnresolvedCapability: Propagated\n            from :class:`SuiteLoader` so the CLI exit-code map still\n            applies (all → 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, suites_dir)\n    active_loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    parsed: ParsedSuite = active_loader.load(manifest_path)\n\n    if eval_id is None:\n        return _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)\n\n    for spec in parsed.evals:\n        if spec.id == eval_id:\n            return _evalspec_to_dict(spec)\n    raise EvalNotFound(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        suite_name=parsed.name,\n        known_ids=sorted(spec.id for spec in parsed.evals),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_yaml(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic YAML document.\n\n    ``sort_keys=False`` preserves the manifest field ordering produced by\n    :func:`_parsed_suite_to_dict` / :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` so the\n    YAML output reads like the source manifest. ``default_flow_style=False``\n    keeps block-style for human review.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return yaml.safe_dump(\n        payload,\n        sort_keys=False,\n        default_flow_style=False,\n        allow_unicode=True,\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_json(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic JSON document.\n\n    ``sort_keys=True`` guarantees byte-level stability across hosts and\n    Python versions (matching the ``eval list --json`` contract).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)\n\n\n@eval_group.command(\"describe\")\n@click.option(\n    \"--suite\",\n    \"suite\",\n    required=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Suite name, filename stem, or path to a manifest. Name lookup \"\n        \"matches the manifest 'name:' field; stem lookup matches \"\n        \"'<suites-dir>/<token>.yaml'.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--eval\",\n    \"eval_id\",\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Filter output to a single post-expansion eval id \"\n        \"(e.g. 'E1' or 'E2.1'). Missing id exits 2 with EvalNotFound.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--json\",\n    \"as_json\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Emit JSON instead of YAML (default is YAML).\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--suites-dir\",\n    \"suites_dir\",\n    type=click.Path(file_okay=False, path_type=Path),\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Override the suite manifest directory used for name/stem \"\n        \"lookups. Defaults to src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/.\"\n    ),\n)\ndef eval_describe(\n    suite: str,\n    eval_id: Optional[str],\n    as_json: bool,\n    suites_dir: Optional[Path],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print a validated, post-parameterize-expansion manifest.\n\n    Output mirrors the validated manifest with no editorial\n    transformation. ``evals[]`` is the post-expansion list so a\n    parameterized row produces one entry per row in the output.\n\n    Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — manifest validated and printed.\n    * ``2`` — schema / id-regex / capability rejection, missing suite\n      (``SuiteNotFound``), or missing eval id (``EvalNotFound``). The\n      stderr line names the typed error class so operators can locate\n      the failure without parsing tracebacks.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    target = suites_dir if suites_dir is not None else _DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR\n\n    try:\n        payload = describe_suite(\n            suite,\n            suites_dir=target,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n        )\n    except SuiteNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n    except EvalNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n    except SuiteLoaderError as exc:  # type: ignore[misc]\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n        sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    if as_json:\n        click.echo(render_describe_json(payload))\n    else:\n        # safe_dump terminates with a trailing newline; click.echo adds\n        # another, so strip to keep output tidy and snapshot-stable.\n        click.echo(render_describe_yaml(payload).rstrip(\"\\n\"))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FR-CLI1 ``eval run`` skeleton (T04.09 / D-0071)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# T04.09 (COMP-001) registers the four-subcommand surface of the ``eval``\n# group: ``run``, ``list``, ``describe``, ``doctor``. The body for ``run``\n# lands in T04.10 (FR-CLI1) along with its twelve flags. Until then this\n# skeleton exists so ``superclaude eval --help`` already lists ``run`` as\n# part of the group surface — operators and downstream callers can rely\n# on the subcommand name and discovery contract even though the\n# functional body is deferred. Invoking ``superclaude eval run`` without\n# the T04.10 body prints a deferral notice and exits 2 (treated as a\n# harness configuration error) so a stray invocation cannot be mistaken\n# for a passing no-op.\n\n\nRUN_BODY_DEFERRED_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Exit code emitted while the FR-CLI1 ``eval run`` body is deferred.\n\nT04.10 lands the real implementation; until then any invocation of\n``superclaude eval run`` exits with this code so CI cannot mistake the\nskeleton for a successful run. Aligned with\n:data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` / :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`\nso the \"harness rejected this invocation\" outcome is uniform across\nsubcommands.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nRUN_BODY_DEFERRED_MESSAGE: str = (\n    \"eval run: FR-CLI1 implementation deferred to T04.10; \"\n    \"see .dev/releases/current/cliEval/phase-4-tasklist.md.\"\n)\n\"\"\"Operator-facing notice printed when the T04.09 skeleton is invoked.\n\nPinned as a module constant so tests can assert the exact line without\nre-deriving the wording on every run. Updating the wording therefore\ntriggers the test suite, preventing silent drift between the skeleton\nand the deferral pointer.\n\"\"\"\n\n\n@eval_group.command(\"run\")\n@click.option(\n    \"--suite\",\n    \"suite\",\n    required=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Suite name, filename stem, or path to a manifest. Same lookup \"\n        \"rules as ``eval describe`` (filesystem path → stem → name field).\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--parallel\",\n    \"parallel\",\n    type=int,\n    default=RunOrchestrator.DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    show_default=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Worker concurrency. Values below 1 clamp to 1; values above 15 \"\n        \"clamp to 15 (design-spec §11).\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--eval\",\n    \"eval_ids\",\n    multiple=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Filter to one or more post-expansion eval ids (e.g. 'E1' or 'E2.1'). \"\n        \"Repeat for multiple ids. Empty selection exits 2 with EvalNotFound.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--no-mcp\",\n    \"no_mcp\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Mark MCP capabilities as skipped-by-flag (FR-CLI1 / FR-G4).\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--no-pty\",\n    \"no_pty\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Run without the vendored PTY harness (degrades stdout capture).\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--output-dir\",\n    \"output_dir\",\n    type=click.Path(file_okay=False, path_type=Path),\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Destination directory for ``summary.{md,json,yaml}`` / ``junit.xml`` / \"\n        \"per-eval artifacts. Resolved through the AC12 scratch-root allowlist. \"\n        \"Defaults to ``.dev/eval-runs/<run-id>/``.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--keep-home\",\n    \"keep_home\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Preserve per-eval HOME directories on PASS (default removes them). \"\n        \"Forwarded to ``EvalRunner.keep_home_on_pass``.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--timeout-mult\",\n    \"timeout_mult\",\n    type=float,\n    default=1.0,\n    show_default=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Multiplier applied to each spec's ``timeout_sec`` before it reaches \"\n        \"``EvalRunner.default_timeout_sec``. Must be > 0.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--max-disk-mb\",\n    \"max_disk_mb\",\n    type=int,\n    default=DEFAULT_DISK_BUDGET_MB,\n    show_default=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Disk-budget ceiling for ``output_dir`` in megabytes. ``0`` disables \"\n        \"the poller entirely (NFR-PERF4).\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--json\",\n    \"as_json\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Emit the run summary as JSON to stdout in addition to writing it to disk.\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--verbose\",\n    \"verbose\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Print a human-readable summary line to stdout when the run finishes.\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--junit\",\n    \"junit\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Also write a JUnit XML report (``junit.xml``) into ``output_dir``.\",\n)\ndef eval_run(\n    suite: str,\n    parallel: int,\n    eval_ids: tuple[str, ...],\n    no_mcp: bool,\n    no_pty: bool,\n    output_dir: Optional[Path],\n    keep_home: bool,\n    timeout_mult: float,\n    max_disk_mb: int,\n    as_json: bool,\n    verbose: bool,\n    junit: bool,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Run a cliEval suite end-to-end (FR-CLI1 / D-0072).\n\n    Wires the twelve FR-CLI1 flags to :class:`RunOrchestrator`,\n    :class:`CapabilityGates`, the :class:`Reporter`, and the\n    :class:`DiskBudgetPoller`. Each spec is executed inside its own\n    per-eval HOME (``HomeIsolation``) under the resolved ``--output-dir``\n    so the AC12 scratch-root allowlist remains the single source of truth\n    for filesystem writes.\n\n    Exit codes (design-spec §4):\n\n    * ``0`` — every expanded eval reached a terminal PASS / SKIPPED /\n      XFAIL outcome and no breach occurred.\n    * ``1`` — at least one eval ended in FAIL, ERRORED, TIMEOUT, or XPASS.\n    * ``2`` — harness-level rejection (invalid flag, scratch-root\n      violation, suite not found, disk-budget exceeded, etc.).\n    * ``3`` — the operator interrupted the run (SIGINT/SIGTERM cooperative\n      cancellation).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Flag validation + clamping (design-spec §11)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    if parallel < RunOrchestrator.MIN_PARALLEL:\n        parallel = RunOrchestrator.MIN_PARALLEL\n    elif parallel > RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL:\n        parallel = RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL\n\n    if timeout_mult <= 0:\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval run: --timeout-mult must be > 0; got {timeout_mult}\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n        sys.exit(HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    if max_disk_mb < 0:\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval run: --max-disk-mb must be >= 0; got {max_disk_mb} \"\n            \"(use 0 to disable the budget)\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n        sys.exit(HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Output dir + AC12 scratch-root allowlist\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    base_config = EvalConfig()\n    run_id = _new_run_id()\n    requested_output = (\n        output_dir if output_dir is not None else _default_output_dir(run_id)\n    )\n\n    try:\n        resolved_output = resolve_scratch_root(\n            requested_output,\n            config=base_config,\n            output_dir=output_dir,\n        )\n    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n        click.echo(format_scratch_root_violation(exc), err=True)\n        sys.exit(SCRATCH_ROOT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    resolved_output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    home_root = resolved_output / \"homes\"\n    home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    # The runtime EvalConfig extends the canonical allowlist with the\n    # per-run home root + the operator-supplied output directory so\n    # downstream ``containment_guard`` calls see a stable allowlist\n    # rather than re-deriving one from scratch.\n    runtime_allowed = tuple(base_config.allowed_scratch_roots) + (\n        resolved_output,\n        home_root,\n    )\n    runtime_config = EvalConfig(\n        paths=base_config.paths,\n        defaults=base_config.defaults,\n        allowed_scratch_roots=runtime_allowed,\n        min_claude_version=base_config.min_claude_version,\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Suite resolution + parse + post-expansion filter\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    try:\n        manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, _DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR)\n    except SuiteNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval run: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    try:\n        parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n    except SuiteLoaderError as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval run: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    manifest_n = len(parsed.evals)\n    specs: tuple[EvalSpec, ...] = parsed.evals\n    if eval_ids:\n        wanted = set(eval_ids)\n        filtered = tuple(s for s in specs if s.id in wanted)\n        missing = wanted - {s.id for s in filtered}\n        if missing:\n            click.echo(\n                f\"eval run: EvalNotFound: no specs match \"\n                f\"{sorted(missing)} in suite={parsed.name!r}\",\n                err=True,\n            )\n            sys.exit(EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n        specs = filtered\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # CapabilityGates (FR-G4): build the gate with the operator's\n    # skip-flag selection so a future SuiteLoader resolver swap can\n    # consume it; the doctor subcommand remains the dedicated HARD-row\n    # checker so we deliberately do NOT call ``check_all()`` here.\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    skip_flags: list[str] = []\n    if no_mcp:\n        skip_flags.append(\"--no-mcp\")\n    if no_pty:\n        skip_flags.append(\"--no-pty\")\n    _gates = CapabilityGates(skip_flags=tuple(skip_flags))\n    del _gates  # construction is the wiring; instance unused at M2.\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Disk-budget poller, cancellation token, signal handler\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    poller = DiskBudgetPoller(\n        resolved_output,\n        max_disk_mb=max_disk_mb,\n        ensure_output_dir=True,\n    )\n    token = CancellationToken()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Per-eval worker closure\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    executor_factory = _resolve_executor_factory()\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        return _run_one_spec(\n            spec,\n            run_dir=resolved_output,\n            home_root=home_root,\n            config=runtime_config,\n            timeout_mult=timeout_mult,\n            keep_home=keep_home,\n            cancellation_token=token,\n            executor_factory=executor_factory,\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Orchestrate\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    started_iso = _utc_iso_now()\n    started_monotonic = time.monotonic()\n\n    orchestrator = RunOrchestrator(\n        run_one=run_one,\n        cancellation_token=token,\n        disk_budget_poller=poller,\n    )\n\n    try:\n        # SignalHandlerInstaller is main-thread-only; the CliRunner-based\n        # tests run on the main thread so this is safe by default.\n        if SignalHandlerInstaller is not None and _can_install_signal_handler():\n            with SignalHandlerInstaller(token):\n                outcomes = orchestrator.run(specs, parallel=parallel)\n        else:\n            outcomes = orchestrator.run(specs, parallel=parallel)\n    except ValueError as exc:\n        # Parallel clamping above means this branch should not fire;\n        # defend in depth anyway so a future refactor never crashes the\n        # CLI with an unhandled ValueError.\n        click.echo(f\"eval run: orchestrator rejected request: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    finished_iso = _utc_iso_now()\n    duration_sec = time.monotonic() - started_monotonic\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Assemble RunSummary + write reports\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    counts, totals = _compute_run_stats(outcomes, manifest_n=manifest_n)\n\n    artifacts: dict[str, str] = {}\n    breach_path = poller.artifact_path() if poller.enabled else None\n    if breach_path is not None:\n        artifacts[DISK_BUDGET_EXCEEDED_ARTIFACT_NAME] = str(breach_path)\n\n    summary = RunSummary(\n        run_id=run_id,\n        started_at=started_iso,\n        finished_at=finished_iso,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        suite=str(manifest_path),\n        manifest_version=parsed.version,\n        parallel=parallel,\n        counts=counts,\n        totals=totals,\n        evals=tuple(outcomes),\n        artifacts=artifacts,\n    )\n\n    Reporter(summary=summary, emit_junit=junit).write(resolved_output)\n\n    # A callable that raises folds\n        # into ERRORED with a partial ``expects`` tuple (whatever\n        # callables ran before the raise).\n        ctx = EvalContext.from_runner_state(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=observed.jsonl_paths,\n            env=exec_ctx.env,\n            exit_code=observed.exit_code,\n            stdout=observed.stdout,\n            stderr=observed.stderr,\n            duration_sec=observed.duration_sec,\n            artifacts=observed.artifacts,\n        )\n\n        for callable_ in expect_callables:\n            try:\n                result = callable_(ctx)\n            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n                raise\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n                state.record_harness_failure(step=\"assert\", exc=exc)\n                return _finalize(\n                    state=state,\n                    home=home,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            state.record_expect(result)\n\n        return _finalize(\n            state=state,\n            home=home,\n            on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n            keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n        )\n\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        # NFR-REL1 contract: cooperative cancellation. Teardown the\n        # partial HOME with ``keep=True`` so forensics survive, then\n        # re-raise so the orchestrator's signal handler can convert this\n        # into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the suite layer.\n        _safe_teardown(home, keep=True, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n        raise\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal state + classification helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LifecycleState:\n    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n\n    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _NO_OBSERVED_RUN\n    expects: list[ExpectResult] = field(default_factory=list)\n    harness_failure_step: Optional[str] = None\n    harness_failure_exc: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,","numLines":350,"startLine":120,"totalLines":1186}}}

The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n        branch this class adds.\n\n        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n\n        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. The run-id encodes the UTC time-of-day (``HHMMSSZ``)\nplus the first 8 hex chars of ``sha256(suite_name + \"\\\\n\" + started_at)``\nso two runs at the same instant against the same suite collide on the\nsame path — the FR-G4 acceptance criterion the phase-4 tasklist pins\n(\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start timestamp + suite name\").\n\nAC12 / scratch-root allowlist\n-----------------------------\n\nThis module only *constructs* paths; it never resolves or validates\nthem against the AC12 (T01.19 / D-0016) scratch-root allowlist. _parse_iso(started_at: str) -> datetime:\n    \"\"\"Parse ``started_at`` as an ISO 8601 instant in UTC.\n\n    Accepts both ``Z`` suffix and ``+00:00`` style offsets. Naive\n    timestamps are interpreted as UTC. The return value is always\n    timezone-aware and normalised to UTC so subsequent ``strftime``\n    calls render the same string across hosts.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not isinstance(started_at, str) or not started_at.strip():\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"started_at must be a non-empty ISO 8601 string; got {started_at!r}\"\n        )\n    s = started_at.strip()\n    if s.endswith(\"Z\"):\n        s = s[:-1] + \"+00:00\"\n    try:\n        dt = datetime.fromisoformat(s)\n    except ValueError as exc:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"started_at {started_at!r} is not a valid ISO 8601 instant: {exc}\"\n        ) from exc\n    if dt.tzinfo is None:\n        dt = dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n    return dt.astimezone(timezone.utc)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Run-id + run-dir composition\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef compose_run_id(started_at: str, suite_name: str = \"\") -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the per-run identifier for ``(started_at, suite_name)``.\n\n    Shape: ``<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`` where the 8-hex tail is the first\n    :data:`_RUN_ID_HASH_LEN` chars of\n    ``sha256(suite_name + \"\\\\n\" + started_at)``.\n\n    The id is deterministic — calling this twice with the same inputs\n    yields the same string — which is exactly the FR-G4 acceptance\n    criterion (\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start timestamp +\n    suite name\"). The hash incorporates ``suite_name`` so two\n    simultaneous runs against different suites cannot collide on the\n    same id.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    dt = _parse_iso(started_at)\n    time_segment = dt.strftime(_TIME_SEGMENT_FMT)\n    digest = hashlib.sha256(\n        (str(suite_name) + \"\\n\" + started_at).encode(\"utf-8\")\n    ).hexdigest()[:_RUN_ID_HASH_LEN]\n    return f\"{time_segment}-{digest}\"\n\n\ndef compose_run_dir(\n    output_root: Path | str,\n    started_at: str,\n    suite_name: str = \"\",\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Return ``<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<run-id>/``.\n\n    The path is **constructed**, not created — the caller is responsible\n    for ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` *after* the AC12 (T01.19)\n    scratch-root allowlist has validated it.\n\n    Arguments\n    ---------\n    output_root:\n        Anchor directory under which the ``.dev/eval-runs/`` subtree is\n        rooted. Operators routinely pass :func:`Path.cwd` so the layout\n        lands inside the repo's canonical AC12 prefix.\n    started_at:\n        ISO 8601 instant marking when the run started. Used for the\n        ``<YYYY-MM-DD>`` date segment + the ``HHMMSSZ`` half of the\n        run-id.\n    suite_name:\n        Stable identifier for the suite being executed (``--suite``\n        value). Folded into the run-id hash so runs against different\n        suites cannot collide.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    dt = _parse_iso(started_at)\n    date_segment = dt.strftime(_DATE_SEGMENT_FMT)\n    run_id = compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)\n    return Path(output_root) / RUN_DIR_PREFIX / date_segment / run_id\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-eval subtree\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass PerEvalPaths:\n    \"\"\"Resolved paths inside a single per-eval subtree.\n\n    Every path is rooted at :attr:`eval_dir`; :attr:`artifacts_dir` is a\n    directory while :attr:`logs_jsonl` and :attr:`tty_transcript` are\n    individual file paths. Consumers should treat the dataclass as the\n    canonical surface for the FR-G4 per-eval contract rather than\n    re-deriving filenames from string constants.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_dir: Path\n    logs_jsonl: Path\n    tty_transcript: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n\n\ndef compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir: Path | str, eval_id: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Return ``<run_dir>/per-eval/<eval_id>/``.\n\n    Validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 character set so a\n    crafted id cannot escape the per-eval subtree via path-traversal\n    components.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not isinstance(eval_id, str) or not _EVAL_ID_RE.match(eval_id):\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"eval_id {eval_id!r} fails the FR-SCH2 [A-Za-z0-9_.-]{{1,64}} guard\"\n        )\n    return Path(run_dir) / PER_EVAL_DIRNAME / eval_id\n\n\ndef allocate_per_eval_paths(\n    run_dir: Path | str,\n    eval_id: str,\n    *,\n    create: bool = True,\n) -> PerEvalPaths:\n    \"\"\"Return + optionally create the per-eval subtree paths.\n\n    When ``create=True`` (the default), the per-eval directory and the\n    ``artifacts/`` subdirectory are created via ``mkdir(parents=True,\n    exist_ok=True)``. The :data:`LOGS_JSONL_NAME` and\n    :data:`TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME` files are *not* touched — they are\n    opened lazily by the runner / PtyDriver on first write.\n\n    Idempotent: re-allocating an existing per-eval subtree returns the\n    same paths and leaves the on-disk state intact.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_dir = compose_per_eval_dir(run_dir, eval_id)\n    artifacts_dir = eval_dir / ARTIFACTS_SUBDIR_NAME\n    paths = PerEvalPaths(\n        eval_dir=eval_dir,\n        logs_jsonl=eval_dir / LOGS_JSONL_NAME,\n        tty_transcript=eval_dir / TTY_TRANSCRIPT_NAME,\n        artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n    )\n    if create:\n        artifacts_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    return paths\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Inverse: parse a run-dir back into (date, run-id)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunDirComponents:\n    \"\"\"Parsed ``(date_segment, run_id)`` pair for a constructed run-dir.\"\"\"\n\n    date_segment: str\n    run_id: str\n\n\ndef parse_run_dir_components(run_dir: Path | str) -> RunDirComponents:\n    \"\"\"Return the ``(date_segment, run_id)`` pair encoded by ``run_dir``.\n\n    Walks the path looking for the ``.dev/eval-runs/`` prefix and takes\n    the next two segments as ``(date, run-id)``. Raises\n    :class:`ValueError` if the path does not match the FR-G4 layout —\n    useful as a structural assertion at the seam between the CLI and\n    the Reporter so a misrouted write fails loudly.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    parts = Path(run_dir).parts\n    # Walk back-to-front so a path like ``/.../.dev/eval-runs/<d>/<r>/...``\n    # still matches even if extra segments trail after the run-id.\n    eval_runs_idx: int | None = None\n    for i in range(len(parts) - 1):\n        if parts[i] == \".dev\" and i + 1 < len(parts) and parts[i + 1] == \"eval-runs\":\n            eval_runs_idx = i + 1\n    if eval_runs_idx is None:\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"run_dir {run_dir!r} is not anchored at .dev/eval-runs/\"\n        )\n    if eval_runs_idx + 2 >= len(parts):\n        raise ValueError(\n            f\"run_dir {run_dir!r} is missing the <date>/<run-id> tail \"\n            \"under .dev/eval-runs/\"\n        )\n    return RunDirComponents(\n        date_segment=parts[eval_runs_idx + 1],\n        run_id=parts[eval_runs_idx + 2],\n    )\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2574,"cache_read_input_tokens":127724,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2574,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":9,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9d8217b5-06b9-4dba-a971-39bb3cf78227","uuid":"5ac4dbe4-510f-496e-a3df-588511b9e949"}

Goal\n\nPin the on-disk shape of every `superclaude eval run` invocation so a\nreviewer (or CI consumer) can locate the artifacts produced by any past\nrun by date + run-id alone — without grep-and-prayer over the\nfilesystem. The layout is the contract every downstream tool (the\nReporter, the per-eval JSONL emitter, the JUnit converter) writes\nagainst.\n\n## 2. Layout\n\n```\n<output_root>/.dev/eval-runs/<YYYY-MM-DD>/<run-id>/\n    summary.md\n    summary.json\n    summary.yaml\n    junit.xml          (only when --junit is set)\n    per-eval/\n        <eval_id>/\n            logs.jsonl\n            tty.transcript\n            artifacts/\n```\n\n| Segment | Source | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| `<output_root>` | `Path.cwd()` by default; `--output-dir` overrides | Anchor under which `.dev/eval-runs/` lives. Default lands inside the canonical AC12 (T01.19 / D-0016) scratch-root allowlist. |\n| `.dev/eval-runs/` | `artifact_layout.RUN_DIR_PREFIX` | Repo-relative prefix. Matches `EvalConfig._default_allowed_scratch_roots`. |\n| `<YYYY-MM-DD>` | UTC ISO date of `started_at` | Per-day bucket so reviewers can navigate by date without exploding `ls`. |\n| `<run-id>` | `compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` | `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>` — see §3. |\n| `summary.{md,json,yaml}` | `Reporter.write()` (COMP-008 / D-0055) | DM-004 aggregated run summary in three formats. |\n| `junit.xml` | `Reporter(emit_junit=True).write()` | Optional; FR-RPT2 feature-gated. |\n| `per-eval/<eval_id>/` | `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` | Self-contained subtree for one eval. |\n| `logs.jsonl` | `EvalRunner` JSONL emitter (T03.05 / D-0049) | Per-eval lifecycle event log. |\n| `tty.transcript` | `PtyDriver` (T02.10 / D-0033) | PTY capture for failure forensics. |\n| `artifacts/` | Per-eval ExpectCallables / executor | Free-form artifact drop for the eval (jsonl logs, screenshots, etc.). |\n\n## 3. Run-id determinism\n\n`compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` returns the same string for the\nsame `(started_at, suite_name)` pair — the FR-G4 acceptance criterion\nthe phase-4 tasklist pins (\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start\ntimestamp + suite name\").\n\n* **Shape:** `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`\n* **Time prefix:** `HHMMSSZ` is the UTC time-of-day of `started_at`.\n  Pure-numeric so the id sorts lexicographically by wall-clock time\n  within a date bucket.\n* **Hash tail:** First 8 hex chars of\n  `sha256(suite_name + \"\\n\" + started_at)`. Folding `suite_name` in\n  guarantees runs against different suites at the same instant land in\n  distinct directories.\n* **Collision risk:** 8 hex chars = 32 bits. The hash is keyed by an\n  ISO-second timestamp, so collisions require two runs *of distinct\n  suites* at the exact same wall-clock second. The CI fleet does not\n  approach this density.\n\n### Why not a random suffix?\n\nA random suffix would prevent reproducibility — the AC explicitly\nrequires determinism so an operator can re-derive the run-id from\n`(started_at, suite_name)` alone (e.g. to look up a historical run\nwithout consulting the run's own manifest).\n\n## 4. Construction vs. creation\n\n`compose_run_dir` and `compose_per_eval_dir` are **path-construction**\nhelpers — they return paths but do not touch disk. Mkdir is the\ncaller's responsibility, and **must happen after**\n`resolve_scratch_root` (the AC12 / T01.19 enforcement boundary)\nvalidates the constructed path against the allowlist.\n\n`allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id, create=True)` is the only\nhelper that creates directories — it mkdir's the per-eval directory and\nthe `artifacts/` subdirectory. The `logs.jsonl` and `tty.transcript`\nfiles are not pre-created; the runner / PtyDriver open them lazily on\nfirst write so an unused eval leaves a clean subtree.\n\n## 5. Routing\n\n* **Reporter writes** (`summary.md` / `summary.json` / `summary.yaml` /\n  `junit.xml`) — routed via `Reporter(summary, emit_junit).write(run_dir)`\n  in `commands.eval_run`. The run_dir is the path\n  `compose_run_dir(...)` constructs when `--output-dir` is unset, or the\n  operator-supplied directory otherwise (in which case the AC12\n  allowlist still gates the resolution).\n* **Per-eval logs** (`logs.jsonl`) — routed via the per-eval worker\n  closure that T04.10 wires up. The worker calls\n  `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` and threads the resulting\n  paths into the `EvalRunner` constructor (`log_dir` override) and the\n  PtyDriver (`transcript_path`).\n* **Eval artifacts** (`per-eval/<eval_id>/artifacts/`) — populated by\n  whatever ExpectCallables or executors choose to drop files there.\n  The directory is pre-created so callers do not need to repeat the\n  `mkdir(parents=True)` boilerplate.\n\n## 6. AC12 interaction\n\n`artifact_layout` constructs paths under `.dev/eval-runs/` so a default\nrun lands inside the canonical AC12 prefix\n(`_default_allowed_scratch_roots` in `config.py`). The CLI body still\ncalls `resolve_scratch_root(requested_output, config=base_config,\noutput_dir=output_dir)` before the first `mkdir`; that call is the\nsingle enforcement boundary. This module does not duplicate the check.\n\nIf the operator passes `--output-dir <X>`, `compose_run_dir` is **not\ncalled** — the CLI uses `X` directly. AC12 then validates `X` exactly\nas it did before T04.13.\n\n## 7. Backwards compatibility\n\n* `--output-dir <X>` continues to write the four summary files directly\n  under `X` (the T04.11 FR-G6 smoke contract).\n* The default behaviour (no `--output-dir`) gains the nested\n  `.dev/eval-runs/<date>/<run-id>/` structure that T04.13 introduces.\n  This is a no-op for callers that already pass `--output-dir`.\n* `EvalRunner` retains its existing\n  `home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl` log destination; T04.10 will\n  add a `log_dir` constructor parameter so the per-run worker can\n  re-route logs into the FR-G4 per-eval subtree without breaking the\n  default-home path that existing tests rely on.\n\n## 8. Acceptance criteria mapping\n\nFrom phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.13:\n\n| AC | Where verified |\n|---|---|\n| Each run produces a directory under `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/` containing `summary.md`, `summary.json`, and a `per-eval/` subtree per eval. | `test_compose_run_dir_shape`, `test_compose_run_dir_anchored_at_run_dir_prefix`, end-to-end via T04.11 smoke once T04.10 wires the worker. |\n| Per-eval subtree contains `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript`, and `artifacts/`. | `test_allocate_per_eval_paths_creates_subtree`. |\n| Run-id is deterministic for a given start timestamp + suite name. | `test_compose_run_id_deterministic_for_same_inputs`, `test_compose_run_id_changes_with_suite_name`, `test_compose_run_id_changes_with_timestamp`. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0074/spec.md` documents the directory layout. | This file. |\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":240,"cache_read_input_tokens":144274,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":240,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"9d8217b5-06b9-4dba-a971-39bb3cf78227","uuid":"f6b1d629-8d35-410d-9b15-d4eba6d51df3"}

Run-id determinism\n\n`compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` returns the same string for the\nsame `(started_at, suite_name)` pair — the FR-G4 acceptance criterion\nthe phase-4 tasklist pins (\"Run-id is deterministic for a given start\ntimestamp + suite name\").\n\n* **Shape:** `<HHMMSSZ>-<8-hex>`\n* **Time prefix:** `HHMMSSZ` is the UTC time-of-day of `started_at`.\n  Pure-numeric so the id sorts lexicographically by wall-clock time\n  within a date bucket.\n* **Hash tail:** First 8 hex chars of\n  `sha256(suite_name + \"\\n\" + started_at)`. Folding `suite_name` in\n  guarantees runs against different suites at the same instant land in\n  distinct directories.\n* **Collision risk:** 8 hex chars = 32 bits. The hash is keyed by an\n  ISO-second timestamp, so collisions require two runs *of distinct\n  suites* at the exact same wall-clock second. The CI fleet does not\n  approach this density.\n\n### Why not a random suffix?\n\nA random suffix would prevent reproducibility — the AC explicitly\nrequires determinism so an operator can re-derive the run-id from\n`(started_at, suite_name)` alone (e.g. to look up a historical run\nwithout consulting the run's own manifest).\n\n## 4. Construction vs. creation\n\n`compose_run_dir` and `compose_per_eval_dir` are **path-construction**\nhelpers — they return paths but do not touch disk. Mkdir is the\ncaller's responsibility, and **must happen after**\n`resolve_scratch_root` (the AC12 / T01.19 enforcement boundary)\nvalidates the constructed path against the allowlist.\n\n`allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id, create=True)` is the only\nhelper that creates directories — it mkdir's the per-eval directory and\nthe `artifacts/` subdirectory. The `logs.jsonl` and `tty.transcript`\nfiles are not pre-created; the runner / PtyDriver open them lazily on\nfirst write so an unused eval leaves a clean subtree.\n\n## 5. Routing\n\n* **Reporter writes** (`summary.md` / `summary.json` / `summary.yaml` /\n  `junit.xml`) — routed via `Reporter(summary, emit_junit).write(run_dir)`\n  in `commands.eval_run`. The run_dir is the path\n  `compose_run_dir(...)` constructs when `--output-dir` is unset, or the\n  operator-supplied directory otherwise (in which case the AC12\n  allowlist still gates the resolution).\n* **Per-eval logs** (`logs.jsonl`) — routed via the per-eval worker\n  closure that T04.10 wires up. The worker calls\n  `allocate_per_eval_paths(run_dir, eval_id)` and threads the resulting\n  paths into the `EvalRunner` constructor (`log_dir` override) and the\n  PtyDriver (`transcript_path`).\n* **Eval artifacts** (`per-eval/<eval_id>/artifacts/`) — populated by\n  whatever ExpectCallables or executors choose to drop files there.\n  The directory is pre-created so callers do not need to repeat the\n  `mkdir(parents=True)` boilerplate.\n\n## 6. AC12 interaction\n\n`artifact_layout` constructs paths under `.dev/eval-runs/` so a default\nrun lands inside the canonical AC12 prefix\n(`_default_allowed_scratch_roots` in `config.py`). The CLI body still\ncalls `resolve_scratch_root(requested_output, config=base_config,\noutput_dir=output_dir)` before the first `mkdir`; that call is the\nsingle enforcement boundary. This module does not duplicate the check.\n\nIf the operator passes `--output-dir <X>`, `compose_run_dir` is **not\ncalled** — the CLI uses `X` directly. AC12 then validates `X` exactly\nas it did before T04.13.\n\n## 7. Backwards compatibility\n\n* `--output-dir <X>` continues to write the four summary files directly\n  under `X` (the T04.11 FR-G6 smoke contract).\n* The default behaviour (no `--output-dir`) gains the nested\n  `.dev/eval-runs/<date>/<run-id>/` structure that T04.13 introduces.\n  This is a no-op for callers that already pass `--output-dir`.\n* `EvalRunner` retains its existing\n  `home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl` log destination; T04.10 will\n  add a `log_dir` constructor parameter so the per-run worker can\n  re-route logs into the FR-G4 per-eval subtree without breaking the\n  default-home path that existing tests rely on.\n\n## 8. Acceptance criteria mapping\n\nFrom phase-4-tasklist.md §T04.13:\n\n| AC | Where verified |\n|---|---|\n| Each run produces a directory under `.dev/eval-runs/<ISO>/<run-id>/` containing `summary.md`, `summary.json`, and a `per-eval/` subtree per eval. | `test_compose_run_dir_shape`, `test_compose_run_dir_anchored_at_run_dir_prefix`, end-to-end via T04.11 smoke once T04.10 wires the worker. |\n| Per-eval subtree contains `logs.jsonl`, `tty.transcript`, and `artifacts/`. | `test_allocate_per_eval_paths_creates_subtree`. |\n| Run-id is deterministic for a given start timestamp + suite name. | `test_compose_run_id_deterministic_for_same_inputs`, `test_compose_run_id_changes_with_suite_name`, `test_compose_run_id_changes_with_timestamp`. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0074/spec.md` documents the directory layout. | This file. |\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
